<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Ann and Ben share $28$ sweets in the ratio 4 : 3

(Ann has $4$ sweets for every $3$ sweets that Ben has). 

How many sweets do they each receive?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the sweets received by Ann $= a$

Sweets received by Ben $= 28 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{Ann} :3 \ \text{Ben}                  &= a \ \text{Ann} : (28 - a) \ \text{Ben} \\\\
\dfrac{4} {3}                                   &= \dfrac {a} {28 - a} \\\\
4 \times (28 - a)                               &= a \times 3 \\\\
112 - 4a                                        &= 3a \\\\
112                                             &= 3a + 4a \\\\
3a + 4a                                         &= 112 \\\\
7a                                              &= 112 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {112} {7} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 16} {7} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 16} {\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                               &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ann}                   &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ben}                   &= 28 - 16 \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16, 12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the sweets received by Ann $= a$
<emptyline>
Sweets received by Ben $= 28 - a$
<emptyline>

4 \times (28 - a)                               &= a \times 3 \\\\

a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 16} {7} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 16} {\cancel 7} \\\\
a                                               &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ann}                   &= 16 \\\\
\text{Sweets received by Ben}                   &= 28 - 16 \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A necklace is made from black and white beads in a ratio of 3 : 2, in other words for every $3$ black beads there were $2$ white beads.

● ● ● ○ ○ ● ● ● ○ ○

If there are $80$ beads altogether, how many beads are black?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let number of black beads $= a$

Number of white beads $= 80 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{black} :2 \ \text{white}    &= a \ \text{black} : (80 - a) \ \text{white} \\\\
\dfrac{3} {2}                         &= \dfrac {a} {80 - a} \\\\
3 \times (80 - a)                     &= a \times 2 \\\\
240 - 3a                              &= 2a \\\\
240                                   &= 2a + 3a \\\\
2a + 3a                               &= 240 \\\\
5a                                    &= 240 \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {240} {5} \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {5 \times 48} {5} \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 48} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                     &= 48
\end{aligned}
$

Number of black beads $= 48$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{whole}                                &:& \text{part} \\\\
= & 5                                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5 \times 16                                 &:& 3 \times 16 \\\\
= & 80                                          &:& 48
\end{array}
$

Number of black beads $= 48$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let number of black beads $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of white beads $= 80 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{black} :2 \ \text{white}    &= a \ \text{black} : (80 - a) \ \text{white} \\\\
\dfrac{3} {2}                         &= \dfrac {a} {80 - a} \\\\
3 \times (80 - a)                     &= a \times 2 \\\\
240 - 3a                              &= 2a \\\\
240                                   &= 2a + 3a \\\\
2a + 3a                               &= 240 \\\\
5a                                    &= 240 \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {240} {5} \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {5 \times 48} {5} \\\\
a                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 48} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                     &= 48
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of black beads $= 48$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{whole}                                &:& \text{part} \\\\
= & 5                                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5 \times 16                                 &:& 3 \times 16 \\\\
= & 80                                          &:& 48
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of black beads $= 48$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Complete the table below to show the percentages of each type of woodpecker.

|    Type A        |    Type B        |    Type C        |
|:------------:    |:------------:    |:------------:    |
| ...........%     | ...........%     | ...........%     |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of type A woodpecker}      &= 6 \times 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 60000 \\\\
\text{Number of type B woodpecker}      &= 10000 \\\\
\text{Number of type C woodpecker}      &= 3 \times 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 30000 \\\\
\text{Total number of woodpeckers}      &= 60000 + 30000 + 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 100000
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of type A woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {60000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 60 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage of type B woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {10000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 10 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage of type C woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {30000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {30000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {30 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \%, 10 \%, 30 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of type A woodpecker}      &= 6 \times 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 60000 \\\\
\text{Number of type B woodpecker}      &= 10000 \\\\
\text{Number of type C woodpecker}      &= 3 \times 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 30000 \\\\
\text{Total number of woodpeckers}      &= 60000 + 30000 + 10000 \\\\
                                        &= 100000
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of type A woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {60000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 60 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage of type B woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {10000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {10 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 10 \% \\\\
\text{Percentage of type C woodpecker}      &= \dfrac {30000} {100000} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {30000 \times 100} {100000} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {30 \cancel {000} \times 1 \cancel {00}} {1 \cancel {000} \cancel {00}} \\\\
                                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

The ratio of **type A : type B** woodpeckers is 6 : 1

                                            &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of water mixed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{screenwash} : 4 \ \text{water}        &= 150 \ \text{screenwash} : a \ \text{water} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {150} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 150 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 600 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of water mixed in screenwash $= 600 \ \text{ml}$

</div>

= & 150 \ \text{ml}                             &:& 600 \ \text{ml}

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of water mixed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
9 \ \text{water} : 1 \ \text{screenwash}        &= 450 \ \text{water} : a \ \text{screenwash} \\\\

a                                               &= \dfrac {450} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 50} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 50} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of screenwash mixed in water $= 50 \ \text{ml}$

= & 450 \ \text{ml}                               &:& 50 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}

<div class='question question'>

$\pounds 50000$ in $\pounds 50$ notes weighs about $1.3 \ \text{Kg}$. How much, **in grams**, does one $\pounds 50$ note weigh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of notes}      &= \dfrac {50000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 \times 1000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel{50} \times 1000} {\cancel{50}} \\\\
                            &= 1000 \\\\
\text{Weight of a note}     &= \dfrac {1.3} { 1000} \ \text{kg} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1000} { 1000} \ \text {g} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} { 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0} } \\\\
                            &= 1.3 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.3 \ \text {g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of notes}      &= \dfrac {50000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 \times 1000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel{50} \times 1000} {\cancel{50}} \\\\
                            &= 1000 \\\\
\text{Weight of a note}     &= \dfrac {1.3} { 1000} \ \text{kg} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1000} { 1000} \ \text {g} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} { 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0} } \\\\
                            &= 1.3 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.3 \ \text {g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the mass of the alloy is $72 \ \text{kg}$, how much iron and tungsten does it contain?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the mass of iron $= a$

Mass of tungsten $= 72 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{iron} : 1 \ \text{tungsten}           &= a \ \text{iron} : (72 - a) \ \text{tungsten} \\\\
\dfrac{5}{1}                                    &= \dfrac {a} {72 - a} \\\\
5 \times (72 - a)                               &= a \\\\
5 \times 72 - 5a                                &= a \\\\
360 - 5a                                        &= a \\\\
360                                             &= 5a + a \\\\
5a + a                                          &= 360 \\\\
6a                                              &= 360 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {360} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 60} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 60} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 60 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of iron}         &= 60 \ \text{kg} \\\\
\text{Mass of tungsten}     &= 72 - 60 \\\\
                            &= 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{alloy}                                &:& \text{iron} \\\\
= & (5 + 1) \ \text{kg}                         &:& 5 \ \text{kg} \\\\
= & 6                                           &:& 5 \\\\
= & 6 \times 12                                 &:& 5 \times 12\\\\
= & 72 \ \text{kg}                              &:& 60 \ \text{kg}
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of iron}         &= 60 \ \text{kg} \\\\
\text{Mass of tungsten}     &= 72 - 60 \\\\
                            &= 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \ \text{kg}, 12 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>

If there is $15 \ \text{kg}$ of iron in a quantity of the alloy, how much tungsten is there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of tungsten $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{iron} : 1 \ \text{tungsten}           &= 15 \ \text{iron} : a \ \text{tungsten} \\\\
5                                               &= \dfrac {15} {a} \\\\
5 \times a                                      &= 15 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 3} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of tungsten $= 3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}

= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {1} {5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 15                                 &:& \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
= & 15                                          &:& \dfrac {5 \times 3} {5} \\\\
= & 15                                          &:& \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5} \\\\
= & 15 \ \text{kg}                              &:& 3 \ \text{kg}
\end{array}
$

Amount of tungsten $= 3 \ \text{kg}$

<div class='question question'>

Five bags of apples cost $\pounds 3.25$.

How much would $\text{seven}$ bags of apples cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of one bag}        &= \pounds \dfrac {3.25} {5} \\\\
\text{Cost of seven bags}     &= 7 \times \dfrac {3.25} {5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 3.25} {5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 325} {5 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 5 \times 65} {5 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel 5 \times 65} {\cancel 5 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 65} {100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {455} {100} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 4.55
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of seven bags of apples $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{bags} : 3.25 \ \text{cost}            &= 7 \ \text{bags} : a \ \text{cost} \\\\
\dfrac {5} {3.25}                               &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
5 \times a                                      &= 7 \times 3.25 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 3.25} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 325} {5 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 5 \times 65} {5 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel 5 \times 65} {\cancel 5 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 65} {100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {455} {100} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 4.55
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of seven bags of apples $= \pounds 4.55$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{apples}       &:& \pounds \\\\
= & 5                   &:& 3.25 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac {3.25} {5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 7          &:& \dfrac {3.25 \times 7} {5} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \dfrac {325 \times 7} {5 \times 100} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \dfrac {5 \times 65 \times 7} {5 \times 100} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 65 \times 7} {\cancel 5 \times 100} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \dfrac {65 \times 7} {100} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \dfrac {455} {100} \\\\
= & 7                   &:& \pounds 4.55
\end{array}
$

Cost of seven bags of apples $= \pounds 4.55$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4.55$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 325} {5 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 5 \times 65} {5 \times 100} \\\\

                              &= \dfrac {7 \times 65} {100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {455} {100} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 4.55
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='uuid'>
<p>F906ACB0-C224-4A7D-99D6-9EEA989C574F</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-5:1:4</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_blocked</p>
</div>

tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A pile of hay can feed $12$ horses for $15$ days.

For how long could the same pile of hay feed $20$ horses? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 15} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5 \times 3} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 15} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5 \times 3} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prccrl question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>1EDD9DF8-7CB7-499A-A11A-243302CCDF2C</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-5:1:7</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prccrl</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

$x$ and $y$ are positive whole numbers. The ratio of $x$ to $y$ is $7:3$. The difference between $x$ and $y$ is $24$.

Find the value of $x$ and the value of $y$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
x - y                                                       &= 24 \\\\
x                                                           &= 24 + y
\end{aligned}
$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
7 \ \text{x} : 3 \ \text{y}                     &= (24 + y) \ \text{x} : y \ \text{y} \\\\
\dfrac{7}{3}                                    &= \dfrac {24 + y} {y} \\\\
7 \times y                                      &= (24 + y) \times 3 \\\\
7y                                              &= 24 \times 3 + 3y \\\\
7y                                              &= 72+ 3y \\\\
7y - 3y                                         &= 72 \\\\
4y                                              &= 72 \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {72} {4} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 18} {4} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 18} {\cancel 4} \\\\
y                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of y to find x.

$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= 24 + y \\\\
            &= 24 + 18 \\\\
            &= 42
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$x= 42,y= 18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x - y                                                       &= 24 \\\\
x                                                           &= 24 + y
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
7 \ \text{x} : 3 \ \text{y}                     &= (24 + y) \ \text{x} : y \ \text{y} \\\\
\dfrac{7}{3}                                    &= \dfrac {24 + y} {y} \\\\
7 \times y                                      &= (24 + y) \times 3 \\\\
7y                                              &= 24 \times 3 + 3y \\\\
7y                                              &= 72+ 3y \\\\
7y - 3y                                         &= 72 \\\\
4y                                              &= 72 \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {72} {4} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 18} {4} \\\\
y                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 18} {\cancel 4} \\\\
y                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's use the value of y to find x.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
x           &= 24 + y \\\\
            &= 24 + 18 \\\\
            &= 42
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$x= 42,y= 18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prccrl question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>DB9B4354-3AB5-437F-B86E-A69BF2EF462D</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2017--sample-paper-6:1:6</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a group of $48$ children, the ratio of boys to girls is $3:5$.

How many boys must join the group to make the ratio of boys to girls $5 : 3$? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of boys before adding new boys $= b$

Number of girls before adding new boys $= 48 - b$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
b \ \text{boys} : (48 - b) \ \text{girls}       &= 3 \ \text{boys} : 5 \ \text{girls} \\\\
\dfrac {b} {48 - b}                             &= \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
b \times 5                                      &= 3 \times (48 - b) \\\\
5b                                              &= 3 \times 48 - 3b \\\\
5b                                              &= 144 - 3b \\\\
5b + 3b                                         &= 144 \\\\
8b                                              &= 144 \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {144} {8} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {8 \times 18} {8} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 18} {\cancel 8} \\\\
b                                               &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of boys}       &= 18 \\\\
\text{Number of girls}      &= 48 - 18 \\\\
                            &= 30
\end{aligned}
$

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{girls}        &:& \text{boys} \\\\
= & 3                   &:& 5 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac{5} {3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times 30} {3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times 3 \times 10} {3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times \cancel 3 \times 10} {\cancel 3} \\\\
= & 30                  &:& 5 \times 10 \\\\
= & 30                  &:& 50
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of boys added}       &= 50 - 18 \\\\
                                  &= 32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of boys before adding new boys $= b$
<emptyline>
Number of girls before adding new boys $= 48 - b$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,

b \times 5                                      &= 3 \times (48 - b) \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

\end{aligned}

$

$
</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

A pile of food can feed $12$ rabbits for $15$ days.

For how long could the same pile of food feed $20$ rabbits? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 15} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5 \times 3} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}     &= \dfrac {12 \times 15} {20} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5 \times 3} {5 \times 4} \\\\
                          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                          &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
                          &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g2 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>F4C49E6C-6442-483E-A6A8-7B917E8F9472</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>oundle-school--11-plus--maths--2018--sample-paper-9:1:6</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_g2</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a class the ratio of boys to girls is 3 : 5.

If there are $6$ less boys than girls, how any pupils are in the class in total? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of girls $= b$

Number of boys $= b - 6$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
(b - 6) \ \text{boys} : b \ \text{girls}        &= 3 \ \text{boys} : 5 \ \text{girls} \\\\
\dfrac {b - 6} {b}                              &= \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
(b - 6) \times 5                                &= 3 \times b \\\\
5b - 30                                         &= 3b \\\\
5b - 3b                                         &= 30 \\\\
2b                                              &= 30 \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {30} {2} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 15} {2} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 15} {\cancel 2} \\\\
b                                               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of girls}      &= 15 \\\\
\text{Number of boys}       &= 15 - 6 \\\\
                            &= 9 \\\\
\text{Total pupils}         &= 15 + 9 \\\\
                            &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of girls $= b$
<emptyline>

5b - 30                                         &= 3b \\\\
5b - 3b                                         &= 30 \\\\
2b                                              &= 30 \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {30} {2} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 15} {2} \\\\
b                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 15} {\cancel 2} \\\\
b                                               &= 15
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

\text{Number of boys}       &= 15 - 6 \\\\
                            &= 9 \\\\

                            &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Bella makes purple paint by mixing blue paint and red paint in the ratio of $4:3$. How much blue paint is needed to make $21$ litres of purple paint?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of blue paint $= b$

Volume of red paint $= 21 - b$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
b \ \text{blue} : (21 - b) \ \text{red}            &= 4 \ \text{blue} : 3 \ \text{red} \\\\
\dfrac {b} {21 - b}                                &= \dfrac {4} {3} \\\\
3 \times b                                         &= 4 \times (21 - b) \\\\
3b                                                 &= 84 - 4b \\\\
3b + 4b                                            &= 84 \\\\
7b                                                 &= 84 \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {84} {7} \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {7 \times 12} {7} \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 12} {\cancel 7} \\\\
b                                                  &= 12 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of blue paint needed $= 12 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple}                                &:& \text{blue} \\\\
= & (4 + 3) \ \text{litres}                      &:& 4 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 7                                            &:& 4 \\\\
= & 7 \times 3                                   &:& 4 \times 3 \\\\
= & 21 \ \text{litres}                           &:& 12 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

Amount of blue paint needed $= 12 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of blue paint $= b$

3 \times b                                         &= 4 \times (21 - b) \\\\

b                                                  &= \dfrac {7 \times 12} {7} \\\\
b                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 7 \times 12} {\cancel 7} \\\\
b                                                  &= 12 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of blue paint needed $= 12 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>

= & 5                   &:& 6 \times 5 \\\\

\end{array}
$

<div class='question question'>

Maya wants to make some purple paint. She knows that she can make purple by mixing red and blue paint in the ratio $4:5$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

She has $12 \ \text{litres}$ of red paint. How much blue paint will she need? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of blue paint she needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{red paint} : 5 \ \text{blue paint}    &= 12 \ \text{red paint} : a \ \text{blue paint} \\\\
\dfrac {4} {5}                                  &= \dfrac {12} {a} \\\\
4 \times a                                      &= 12 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 15 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of blue paint she needed $= 15 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{red paint}    &:& \text{blue paint} \\\\
= & 4                   &:& 5 \\\\
= & 1                   &:& \dfrac{5} {4} \\\\
= & 1 \times 12         &:& \dfrac{5 \times 12} {4} \\\\
= & 12                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times 4 \times 3} {4} \\\\
= & 12                  &:& \dfrac{5 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 4} \\\\
= & 12                  &:& 5 \times 3 \\\\
= & 12 \ \text{litres}  &:& 15 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

Amount of blue paint she needed $= 15 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$15 \text{ litres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the amount of blue paint she needed $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

4 \times a                                      &= 12 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {12 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 3 \times 5} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 3 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 15 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of blue paint she needed $= 15 \ \text{litres}$
</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much purple paint will that make? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total purple paint} &= 12 + 15 \\\\
                          &= 27 \text{ litres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Eliot needs $36 \ \text{litres}$ of purple paint. How much red paint will he need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of red paint $= a$

Volume of blue paint $= 36 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \ \text{red paint} : 5 \ \text{blue paint}     &= a \ \text{red paint} : (36 - a) \ \text{blue paint} \\\\
\dfrac {4} {5}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {36 - a} \\\\
4 \times (36 - a)                               &= 5 \times a \\\\
4 \times 36 - 4a                                &= 5a \\\\
144 - 4a                                        &= 5a \\\\
144                                             &= 5a + 4a \\\\
5a + 4a                                         &= 144 \\\\
9a                                              &= 144 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {144} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 16} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 16} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 16 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of red paint he needed $= 16 \ \text{litres}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple paint}       &:& \text{red paint} \\\\
= & (4 + 5) \ \text{litres}   &:& 4 \ \text{litres} \\\\
= & 9                         &:& 4 \\\\
= & 9 \times 4                &:& 4 \times 4 \\\\
= & 36 \ \text{litres}        &:& 16 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

Amount of red paint he needed $= 16 \ \text{litres}$

</div>

4 \times (36 - a)                               &= 5 \times a \\\\
4 \times 36 - 4a                                &= 5a \\\\
144 - 4a                                        &= 5a \\\\
144                                             &= 5a + 4a \\\\
5a + 4a                                         &= 144 \\\\
9a                                              &= 144 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {144} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 16} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 16} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 16 \ \text{litres}
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On the plan, the garden is $2  \ \text{cm}$ wide. What is the real width of the garden?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the actual width of garden $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{map} : 12 \ \text{actual}             &= 2 \ \text{map} : a \ \text{actual} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {12}                                 &= \dfrac {2} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= 24 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

Real width of the garden $= 24 \ \text{m}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{map}          &:& \text{actual} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{cm}       &:& 12 \ \text{m} \\\\
= & 2                   &:& 12 \times 2 \\\\
= & 2                   &:& 24 \ \text{m}
\end{array}
$

Real width of the garden $= 24 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24 \ \text{m}$

</div>

1 \times a                                      &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= 24 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>

<emptyline>

</div>

<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2015--sample-paper-6:1:8</p>

<div class='question question'>

*Purple Fire* paint is made by mixing red and blue paint in the ratio $3:1$. If I want to make $1 \ \text{litre}$ of *Purple Fire* paint, how much red paint do I need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of red paint $= a$

Volume of blue paint $= 1000 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{red} : 1 \ \text{blue}                &= a \ \text{red} : (1000 - a) \ \text{blue} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {1}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {1000 - a} \\\\
3 \times (1000 - a)                             &= a \\\\
3000 - 3a                                       &= a \\\\
3000                                            &= a + 3a \\\\
a + 3a                                          &= 3000 \\\\
4a                                              &= 3000 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3000} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 750} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 750} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 750 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of red paint needed $= 750 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple paint}                         &:& \text{red paint} \\\\
= & (1 + 3) \ \text{ml}                         &:& 3 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 4                                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 4 \times 250                                &:& 3 \times 250 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{ml}                            &:& 750 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{litres}                           &:& 750 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Amount of red paint needed $= 750 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$750 \ \text{ml}$ 

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of red paint $= a$
<emptyline>
Volume of blue paint $= 1000 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{red} : 1 \ \text{blue}                &= a \ \text{red} : (1000 - a) \ \text{blue} \\\\
\dfrac {3} {1}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {1000 - a} \\\\
3 \times (1000 - a)                             &= a \\\\
3000 - 3a                                       &= a \\\\
3000                                            &= a + 3a \\\\
a + 3a                                          &= 3000 \\\\
4a                                              &= 3000 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {3000} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 750} {4} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 750} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                               &= 750 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of red paint needed $= 750 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{purple paint}                         &:& \text{red paint} \\\\
= & (1 + 3) \ \text{ml}                         &:& 3 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 4                                           &:& 3 \\\\
= & 4 \times 250                                &:& 3 \times 250 \\\\
= & 1000 \ \text{ml}                            &:& 750 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{litres}                           &:& 750 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of red paint needed $= 750 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$750 \ \text{ml}$ 
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the cost of one ticket.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of one ticket}     &= \dfrac {21.25} {17} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {2125} {17 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {17 \times 125} {17 \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {17} \times 125} {\cancel {17} \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {125} {100} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 1.25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {17} \times 125} {\cancel {17} \times 100} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {125} {100} \\\\
                              &= \pounds 1.25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the cost of seven tickets.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using value from the above questions,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 17 tickets}       &= 7 \times 1.25 \\\\
                                &= 7 \times \dfrac {125} {100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {7 \times 125} {100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {875} {100} \\\\
                                &= \pounds 8.75
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Let the cost of $7$ tickets $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
17 \ \text{quantity} : 21.25 \ \text{cost}      &= 7 \ \text{quantity} : a \ \text{cost} \\\\
\dfrac {17} {\pounds 21.25}                     &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
\dfrac {17} {2125 \text{p}}                     &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
\dfrac {17} {2125}                              &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
17 \times a                                     &= 7 \times 2125 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 2125} {17} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 17 \times 125} {17} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times \cancel {17} \times 125} {\cancel {17}} \\\\
a                                               &= 7 \times 125 \\\\
a                                               &= 875 \text{p} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 8.75
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of $7$ tickets $= \pounds 8.75$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$

= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {2125} {17} \\\\
= & 1 \times 7                                  &:& \dfrac {2125 \times 7} {17} \\\\
= & 7                                           &:& \dfrac {17 \times 125 \times 7} {17} \\\\
= & 7                                           &:& \dfrac {\cancel {17} \times 125 \times 7} {\cancel {17}} \\\\
= & 7                                           &:& 125 \times 7 \\\\
= & 7                                           &:& 875 \text{p} \\\\
= & 7                                           &:& \pounds 8.75
\end{array}
$

Cost of $7$ tickets $= \pounds 8.75$

</div>

<div class='question question'>

A drink contains lime, orange and apple juices in the ratio $2:7:6$. Find the volume of orange juice contained in $300 \ \text{ml}$ of the drink.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of orange juice $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
(2 + 7 + 6) \ \text{drink} : 7 \ \text{orange}  &= 300 \ \text{drink} : a \ \text{orange} \\\\
\dfrac {2 + 7 + 6} {7}                          &= \dfrac {300} {a} \\\\
\dfrac {15} {7}                                 &= \dfrac {300} {a} \\\\
15 \times a                                     &= 300 \times 7 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {300 \times 7} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 15 \times 7} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {15} \times 7} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
a                                               &= 20 \times 7 \\\\
a                                               &= 140 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Volume of orange juice $= 140 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{drink}        &:& \text{orange} \\\\
= & 2 + 7 + 6           &:& 7 \\\\
= & 15                  &:& 7 \\\\
= & 15 \times 20        &:& 7 \times 20 \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{ml}     &:& 140 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Volume of orange juice $= 140 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$140 \text { ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of orange juice $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
(2 + 7 + 6) \ \text{drink} : 7 \ \text{orange}  &= 300 \ \text{drink} : a \ \text{orange} \\\\
\dfrac {2 + 7 + 6} {7}                          &= \dfrac {300} {a} \\\\
\dfrac {15} {7}                                 &= \dfrac {300} {a} \\\\
15 \times a                                     &= 300 \times 7 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {300 \times 7} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times 15 \times 7} {15} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {15} \times 7} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
a                                               &= 20 \times 7 \\\\
a                                               &= 140 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Volume of orange juice $= 140 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{drink}        &:& \text{orange} \\\\
= & 2 + 7 + 6           &:& 7 \\\\
= & 15                  &:& 7 \\\\
= & 15 \times 20        &:& 7 \times 20 \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{ml}     &:& 140 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Volume of orange juice $= 140 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$140 \text { ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>

$

  & \text{doughnuts}                  &:& \text{cost} \\\\

= & 12                                &:& \pounds 17.40
\end{array}
$

Cost $= \pounds 17.40$

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much water should be mixed with $30 \ \text{ml}$ of cordial?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of water $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{cordial} : 4 \ \text{water}           &= 30 \ \text{cordial} : a \ \text{water} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {30} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 30 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of water $= 120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cordial}                          &:& \text{water} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{ml}                           &:& 4 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 30                                      &:& 4 \times 30 \\\\
= & 3 \ \text{ml}                           &:& 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Amount of water $= 120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of water $= a$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}

\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {30} {a} \\\\
1 \times a                                      &= 30 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of water $= 120 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cordial}                          &:& \text{water} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{ml}                           &:& 4 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 30                                      &:& 4 \times 30 \\\\
= & 3 \ \text{ml}                           &:& 120 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of water $= 120 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much cordial should be used to make $750 \ \text{ml}$ of the drink?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the volume of cordial used $= a$

Volume of water used $= 750 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{cordial} : 4 \ \text{water}           &= a \ \text{cordial} : (750 - a)) \ \text{water} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {750 - a} \\\\
750 - a                                         &= 4a \\\\
750                                             &= 4a + a \\\\
4a + a                                          &= 750 \\\\
5a                                              &= 750 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {750} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 150} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 150} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 150 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of water $= 150 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{drink}                            &:& \text{cordial} \\\\
= & (1 + 4) \ \text{ml}                     &:& 1 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 1 \\\\
= & 5 \times 150                            &:& 1 \times 150 \\\\
= & 750 \ \text{ml}                         &:& 150 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$

Amount of water $= 150 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$150 \ \text{ml}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the volume of cordial used $= a$
<emptyline>
Volume of water used $= 750 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{cordial} : 4 \ \text{water}           &= a \ \text{cordial} : (750 - a)) \ \text{water} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {750 - a} \\\\
750 - a                                         &= 4a \\\\
750                                             &= 4a + a \\\\
4a + a                                          &= 750 \\\\
5a                                              &= 750 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {750} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 150} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 150} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 150 \ \text{ml}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of water $= 150 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{drink}                            &:& \text{cordial} \\\\
= & (1 + 4) \ \text{ml}                     &:& 1 \ \text{ml} \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 1 \\\\
= & 5 \times 150                            &:& 1 \times 150 \\\\
= & 750 \ \text{ml}                         &:& 150 \ \text{ml}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Amount of water $= 150 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$150 \ \text{ml}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-10:1:8</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>

<div class='topics'>

<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a fruit yoghurt weighing $117 \ \text{g}$, the ratio of weight of fruit to weight of yoghurt is $2:7$. Calculate the weight of fruit.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of fruit $= a$

Weight of yoghurt $= 117 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{fruit} : 7 \ \text{yoghurt}           &= a \ \text{fruit} : (117 - a) \ \text{yoghurt} \\\\
\dfrac {2} {7}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {117 - a} \\\\
2 \times (117 - a)                              &= 7a \\\\
2 \times 117 - 2a                               &= 7a \\\\
234 - 2a                                        &= 7a \\\\
234                                             &= 7a + 2a \\\\
2a + 7a                                         &= 234 \\\\
9a                                              &= 234 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {234} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 26} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 26} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 26 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Weight of the fruit $= 26 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{mixture}                              &:& \text{fruit} \\\\
= & (2 + 7) \ \text{g}                          &:& 2 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & (2 + 7)                                     &:& 2 \\\\
= & 9                                           &:& 2 \\\\
= & 9 \times 13                                 &:& 2 \times 13 \\\\
= & 117 \ \text{g}                              &:& 26 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Weight of the fruit $= 26 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$26 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of fruit $= a$
<emptyline>
Weight of yoghurt $= 117 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{fruit} : 7 \ \text{yoghurt}           &= a \ \text{fruit} : (117 - a) \ \text{yoghurt} \\\\
\dfrac {2} {7}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {117 - a} \\\\
2 \times (117 - a)                              &= 7a \\\\
2 \times 117 - 2a                               &= 7a \\\\
234 - 2a                                        &= 7a \\\\
234                                             &= 7a + 2a \\\\
2a + 7a                                         &= 234 \\\\
9a                                              &= 234 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {234} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 26} {9} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 9 \times 26} {\cancel 9} \\\\
a                                               &= 26 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Weight of the fruit $= 26 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{mixture}                              &:& \text{fruit} \\\\
= & (2 + 7) \ \text{g}                          &:& 2 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & (2 + 7)                                     &:& 2 \\\\
= & 9                                           &:& 2 \\\\
= & 9 \times 13                                 &:& 2 \times 13 \\\\
= & 117 \ \text{g}                              &:& 26 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Weight of the fruit $= 26 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>44F0E0BB-97E5-4A28-BD0C-66D1683C7133</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>sevenoaks-school--11-plus--maths--2019--sample-paper-10:1:9</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A bottle of blackcurrant cordial makes enough drink to fill $60$ glasses when it is diluted in the ratio $1 \ \text{part}$ cordial to $4 \ \text{parts}$ water. 
How many glasses of drink would a bottle of cordial make if it is diluted in the ratio $1 \ \text{part}$ cordial to $5 \ \text{parts}$ water?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of glasses of cordial used $= a$

Number of glasses of water used $= 60 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \ \text{cordial} : 4 \ \text{water}           &= a \ \text{cordial} : (60 - a)) \ \text{water} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4}                                  &= \dfrac {a} {60 - a} \\\\
60 - a                                          &= 4a \\\\
60                                              &= 4a + a \\\\
4a + a                                          &= 60 \\\\
5a                                              &= 60 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {60} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 12} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cordial}                          &:& \text{water} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& 5 \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 5 \times 12 \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 60
\end{array}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of glasses of drink}       &= 12 + 60 \\\\
                                        &= 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of glasses of cordial used $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of glasses of water used $= 60 - a$
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}

a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 12} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{cordial}                          &:& \text{water} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& 5 \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 5 \times 12 \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 60
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
$

                                        &= 72
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$72$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

William changes $\pounds 240$ into Dollars at the beginning of his holiday. How many Dollars does he receive?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of dollars he received $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \ \text{pounds} : 5 \ \text{dollars}          &= 240 \ \text{pounds} : a \ \text{dollars} \\\\
\dfrac{3} {5}                                   &= \dfrac {240} {a} \\\\
3 \times a                                      &= 240 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {240 \times 5} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {80 \times 3 \times 5} {3} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {80 \times \cancel 3 \times 5} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                                               &= 80 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= 400 \ \text{dollars}
\end{aligned}
$

Amount of dollars he received $= 400$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \pounds                                 &:& \text{dollar} \\\\
= & 3                                       &:& 5 \\\\
= & 3 \times 80                             &:& 5 \times 80 \\\\
= & 240                                     &:& 400
\end{array}
$

Amount of dollars he received $= 400$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text{dollar} \ 400$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

He spends $150$ Dollars on a coat. How many pounds is this?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount in pounds $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{dollars} : 3 \ \text{pounds}          &= 150 \ \text{dollars} : a \ \text{pounds} \\\\
\dfrac{5} {3}                                   &= \dfrac {150} {a} \\\\
5 \times a                                      &= 150 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {150 \times 3} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {30 \times 5 \times 3} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 30 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 90
\end{aligned}
$

Amount in pounds $= \pounds 90$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{dollar}                           &:& \pounds \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5 \times 30                             &:& 3 \times 30 \\\\
= & 150                                     &:& \pounds 90
\end{array}
$

Amount in pounds $= \pounds 90$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

He spends $55$ Dollars on a pair of training shoes. How many pounds is this?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount in pounds $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{dollars} : 3 \ \text{pounds}          &= 55 \ \text{dollars} : a \ \text{pounds} \\\\
\dfrac{5} {3}                                   &= \dfrac {55} {a} \\\\
5 \times a                                      &= 55 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {55 \times 3} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {11 \times 5 \times 3} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 11 \times 3 \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 33
\end{aligned}
$

Amount in pounds $= \pounds 33$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{dollar}                           &:& \pounds \\\\
= & 5                                       &:& 3 \\\\
= & 5 \times 11                             &:& 3 \times 11 \\\\
= & 55                                      &:& \pounds 33
\end{array}
$

Amount in pounds $= \pounds 33$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>74769273-0862-4A60-84DF-948A3F1CC2E6</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>st-albans-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-1:1:18</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>

<div class='question question'>

Pairs of avocados are sold in a shop. $16$ pairs of avocados are sold at a total cost of $\pounds 24$. The shopkeeper now decides to separate the pairs and sell the avocados individually, but without any change to the total price. What is the cost of an individual avocado? Give your answer in pence.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 pair of avacoda}      &= \pounds \dfrac {24} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {16} \ \text{pence} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {2400} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {16 \times 150} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 150} {\cancel {16}} \\\\
                                      &= 150 \ \text{pence}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 avacoda}      &= \dfrac {150} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {2 \times 75} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                              &= 75 \ \text{pence}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75 \ \text{pence}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 pair of avacoda}      &= \pounds \dfrac {24} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {24 \times 100} {16} \ \text{pence} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {2400} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {16 \times 150} {16} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 150} {\cancel {16}} \\\\
                                      &= 150 \ \text{pence}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 1 avacoda}      &= \dfrac {150} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {2 \times 75} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 75} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                              &= 75 \ \text{pence}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$75 \ \text{pence}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_pr rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>2E70DD3A-FFFA-4301-9A23-47091DF822AE</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>st-albans-high-school-for-girls--11-plus--maths--2016--sample-paper-1:1:26</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Md Rameez</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_mr_pr</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tratio
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Jason and Patrick win a prize of $\pounds 75$ for their entry into an inventing competition. For every $2$ hours that Patrick worked on the project Jason worked $3$, so it is decided that for every $\pounds 2$ that Patrick receives Jason should receive $\pounds 3$. How much will Jason and Patrick receive? Write your 
answers in the boxes below. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the money received by Jason $= a$

Money received by Patrick $= 75 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{Patrick} : 3 \ \text{Jason}           &= (75 - a) \ \text{Patrick} : a \ \text{Jason} \\\\
\dfrac {2} {3}                                  &= \dfrac {75 - a} {a} \\\\
2a                                              &= 3 \times (75 - a) \\\\
2a                                              &= 3 \times 75 - 3a \\\\
2a                                              &= 225 - 3a \\\\
2a + 3a                                         &= 225 \\\\
5a                                              &= 225 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {225} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 45} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 45} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 45
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money received by Jason}        &= \pounds 45 \\\\
\text{Money received by Patrick}      &= 75 - 45 \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 30
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 45, \pounds 30$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the money received by Jason $= a$
<emptyline>
Money received by Patrick $= 75 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{Patrick} : 3 \ \text{Jason}           &= (75 - a) \ \text{Patrick} : a \ \text{Jason} \\\\
\dfrac {2} {3}                                  &= \dfrac {75 - a} {a} \\\\
2a                                              &= 3 \times (75 - a) \\\\
2a                                              &= 3 \times 75 - 3a \\\\
2a                                              &= 225 - 3a \\\\
2a + 3a                                         &= 225 \\\\
5a                                              &= 225 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {225} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 45} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 45} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= \pounds 45
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money received by Jason}        &= \pounds 45 \\\\
\text{Money received by Patrick}      &= 75 - 45 \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 30
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 45, \pounds 30$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\end{array}

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On a ferry to France, I wanted to buy a large bar of Toblerone 
chocolate, which cost $\pounds 3$. How many Euros would I have to pay for 
it?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the euros $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 1 : 1.2 \ \text{Euros}                  &= \pounds 3 : a \ \text{Euros} \\\\
\dfrac{1}{1.2}                                  &= \dfrac {3} {a} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \times 1.2 \\\\
a                                               &= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}
\end{aligned}
$

Payment $= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \pounds                 &:& \text{Euros} \\\\
= & 1                       &:& 1.2 \\\\
= & 1 \times 3              &:& 1.2 \times 3 \\\\
= & \pounds 3               &:& 3.6 \ \text{Euros}
\end{array}
$

Payment $= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3.6 \ \text{Euro}$

</div>

a                                               &= 3 \times 1.2 \\\\
a                                               &= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Payment $= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}$
</code></pre>

= & 1                       &:& 1.2 \\\\
= & 1 \times 3              &:& 1.2 \times 3 \\\\
= & \pounds 3               &:& 3.6 \ \text{Euros}
\end{array}

<emptyline>
Payment $= 3.6 \ \text{Euros}$
